### Directory Expansion III (v2.7) — 2,061 Further Named Techniques

*Added in v2.7, and the last expansion the directory needs: with it the catalogue stands at 2,500 named techniques. Entry codes continue from Expansion II: Novice from N-EM-46 / N-GR-34 / N-ST-33 / N-WK-23, Journeyman from J-88, Adept from AD-75, Artisan from AR-60, Master from M-35, Warden from W-26, Legend from LG-19, Ascension from AS-11. Prevalence weighting follows §3.3's rarity table as before: Novice 800, Journeyman 300, Adept 360, Artisan 260, Master 160, Warden 120, Legend 40, Beyond Legend 21. Sovereign remains uncatalogued by design (§4.1–§4.3). One thing is new: every entry in the directory, founding and expansion alike, now has a* working form *— its tier's base equation specialised to it — numbered Eq. 8.1–8.2500 in directory order and housed in `codex/working-equations.md` (§8). Each entry below cites its own.*

#### How to read this expansion

All the earlier reading rules carry forward unchanged: single-channel `delta(F_f)` distortions (or sourceless passive reads) at Novice; Eq. 4.13's disjoint windows at Journeyman, each entry naming its two Novice components; only the six `Chi(f1,f2)` pairs at Adept, each entry naming its pair; one signature material per Artisan entry (Eq. 3.1e, 4.16) with its Eq. 4.17 neighbours named; complete-`S` workings at Master (Eq. 4.18–4.19); one proven geometry per Warden entry (Eq. 4.20–4.21); institutions at Legend (Eq. 4.22–4.23); fragments toward the four paths at Beyond Legend (Eq. 4.24–4.27). A working form adds one thing to its base equation: a shape factor `eta_<code>` (defined in §5) whose meaning the form states in words. Where an entry mentions `t_hold`, `theta_arc`, `T_pulse` or `D_cycle`, those are §5's directory-local symbols.

---

#### Novice (Common) — 800 entries

*Electromagnetic (`k_EM`)* — extends Eq. 4.0a; passive reads extend Eq. 4.0e.

**[N-EM-46] Signal Key**
Spark Draw's discharge (N-EM-01) sized and aimed for a fouled flint, pulsed on a period `T_pulse` rather than held. Taught to every apprentice in garrison towns, and mastered by fewer than the guild admits. Past the reach of the discharge there is nothing at all. Compare N-EM-05, examined in the same hour and confused with it by nobody who has cast either.

**[N-EM-47] The Candle Seam**
Spark Draw's discharge (N-EM-01) sized and aimed for a beacon on a headland. Cast, in border towns, more often by children than the guild would like. It startles and kindles; it has never once wounded, and the entry is proud of that. Compare N-EM-45, examined in the same hour and confused with it by nobody who has cast either.

**[N-EM-48] Lantern Palm**
Eq. 4.0a laid into a stone floor: heat put in where it is wanted and nowhere else, held over `t_hold`. The technique behind half the warm rooms in coaching country and none of the stories about them. Nothing it does would count as heating at any real scale. Holders of N-EM-08 learn it in an afternoon; the reverse is not true.

**[N-EM-49] Hearth Road**
Eq. 4.0a held low and long over a wall of green plaster until it is dry through. Common in quarry villages; examiners set it because it is easy to attempt and hard to do well. It dries; it does not cure or season, whatever the trade hopes. The directory files it beside N-EM-41 and lets the two argue.

**[N-EM-50] Small Seam**
Eq. 4.0a laid into a kettle: heat put in where it is wanted and nowhere else, held over `t_hold`. A night-watche's technique, and a vintner's, and disputed between them at every fair. It does not cook, forge or cure; it warms, and stops. Eq. 8.444 gives the number; the trade gives the name.

**[N-EM-51] Grey Vigil**
Spark Draw's discharge (N-EM-01) sized and aimed for a bell-wire. Cast, in the lake settlements, more often by children than the guild would like. Past the reach of the discharge there is nothing at all. Holders of N-EM-15 learn it in an afternoon; the reverse is not true.

**[N-EM-52] Touch of Kinds**
Eq. 4.0a run in reverse over a jelly: heat drawn out, gently, for as long as the caster holds it. The technique behind half the warm rooms in cathedral cities and none of the stories about them. It chills; it does not freeze, and a holder who tries learns why Fid_min exists. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to N-EM-08.

**[N-EM-53] Filament Seam**
Spark Draw's discharge (N-EM-01) sized and aimed for a lantern across a yard. Night-watches pay for it by the hour and complain about the price with real affection. Past the reach of the discharge there is nothing at all. Not to be confused with N-EM-04, which is louder and less useful.

**[N-EM-54] Blush of Lamps**
Lumen Thread's held glow (N-EM-03) shaped for a ship's binnacle, pulsed on a period `T_pulse` rather than held. Smiths keep a holder on retainer through the winter and forget them by spring. It lights; it does not burn, and holders who try both learn why Fid_min exists. Eq. 8.448 gives the number; the trade gives the name.

**[N-EM-55] Quiet Round**
Lumen Thread's held glow (N-EM-03) shaped for a loom, shaped to an arc `theta_arc` and dark outside it. Potters in the dye-works quarter cast it daily and would sooner lose a knife. The glow is honest about its source: a held ripple, not a flame. Everything the entry does is in Eq. 8.449; everything it does not do is in the shape factor.

**[N-EM-56] Sun's Ring**
Eq. 4.0a laid into a soldering iron: heat put in where it is wanted and nowhere else, held over `t_hold`. A fishmonger's first working after Spark Draw, and the one they are still casting at sixty. It does not cook, forge or cure; it warms, and stops. Everything the entry does is in Eq. 8.450; everything it does not do is in the shape factor.

**[N-EM-57] The Small Vigil**
Eq. 4.0a laid into a wax pot: heat put in where it is wanted and nowhere else, held over `t_hold`. Cast, in the sheep uplands, more often by children than the guild would like. It does not cook, forge or cure; it warms, and stops. The working form, Eq. 8.451, is short; the practice behind it is not.

**[N-EM-58] Copper's Line**
A low Eq. 4.0a held for hours over a lock-keeper's hut, judged by what does not get cold, held over `t_hold`. Midwives keep a holder on retainer through the winter and forget them by spring. It keeps warm; it does not make warm, and a cold room stays a cold room. Its working form (Eq. 8.452) is the one most apprentices learn to read first.

The working form is Eq. 8.593; the shape factor does the rest.

**[N-EM-200] Kiln Coax**
Eq. 4.0a run in reverse over a surgeon's tray: heat drawn out, gently, for as long as the caster holds it, held over `t_hold`. Taught to every apprentice in quarry villages, and mastered by fewer than the guild admits. Nothing it does would count as preservation at any real timescale. Everything the entry does is in Eq. 8.594; everything it does not do is in the shape factor.

**[N-EM-201] Still's Key**
Eq. 4.0a held low and long over a rick of hay until it is dry through, held over `t_hold`. A vintner's technique, and a apothecarie's, and disputed between them at every fair. It dries; it does not cure or season, whatever the trade hopes. Compare N-EM-198, examined in the same hour and confused with it by nobody who has cast either.

It startles and kindles; it has never once wounded, and the entry is proud of that. Examiners pair it with N-EM-11 and mark the transition, not the castings.

**[N-EM-203] Long Bloom**

It keeps warm; it does not make warm, and a cold room stays a cold room. The working form is Eq. 8.597; the shape factor does the rest.

*Gravity (`k_grav`)* — extends Eq. 4.0b.

**[N-GR-34] Anvil's Bridge**
Eq. 4.0b laid under a surgeon's patient: weight partly cancelled for the length of the carry, held over `t_hold`. Taught to ostlers early, since the alternative is a bad back by thirty. Past the ripple's reach the thing weighs what it weighs. The working form is Eq. 8.660; the shape factor does the rest.

**[N-GR-35] Level's Drift**
Eq. 4.0b held just short of full cancellation under a chart weight, held over `t_hold`. Cast in quarry villages wherever something heavy has to go somewhere awkward. It carries the load's edge, not the load, and the caster still sweats. Its shape factor is small, and the entry is honest about it.

**[N-GR-36] Anvil Fall**
Eq. 4.0b in reverse over a wobbling ladder: weight added where it is wanted, briefly. Taught to shepherds early, since the alternative is a bad back by thirty. A dropped hold is a dropped load; the examination includes one. Holders of N-GR-32 learn it in an afternoon; the reverse is not true.

**[N-GR-37] Heavy Yoke**
Eq. 4.0b laid under a bridge timber: weight partly cancelled for the length of the carry. Standard among hod-carriers; the examination is a loaded cart and a steep lane. It carries the load's edge, not the load, and the caster still sweats. Compare N-GR-28, examined in the same hour and confused with it by nobody who has cast either.

**[N-GR-38] Fair's Wake**
Eq. 4.0b in reverse over a stack of hides: weight added where it is wanted, briefly. Hired in a market town by the load; the rate is posted at the yard gate. It lightens; it never lifts, and the difference is a broken foot. Eq. 8.664 gives the number; the trade gives the name.

**[N-GR-39] Grey Trust**
Eq. 4.0b laid under a bridge timber: weight partly cancelled for the length of the carry. Taught to roofers early, since the alternative is a bad back by thirty. The weight returns the instant the hold ends, and the trade plans for it. Not to be confused with N-GR-08, which is louder and less useful.

**[N-GR-40] High's Wake**
Eq. 4.0b laid along a grain chute so what should run, runs, held over `t_hold`. Standard among shepherds; the examination is a loaded cart and a steep lane. Nothing about it is flight; the directory has said so since N-GR-01. Everything the entry does is in Eq. 8.666; everything it does not do is in the shape factor.

**[N-GR-41] Heavy Hand**
Eq. 4.0b laid under a loaded hod: weight partly cancelled for the length of the carry. Standard among riggers; the examination is a loaded cart and a steep lane. A dropped hold is a dropped load; the examination includes one. Compare N-GR-36, examined in the same hour and confused with it by nobody who has cast either.

**[N-GR-42] Slack Hoist**
A whisper of Eq. 4.0b under a rope bridge so it settles rather than sways, held over `t_hold`. A cooper's daily working, and a nurse's occasional one. A dropped hold is a dropped load; the examination includes one. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to N-GR-02.

**[N-GR-43] Deep Yoke**
Eq. 4.0b in reverse over a mooring stone: weight added where it is wanted, briefly. Hired in the dye-works quarter by the load; the rate is posted at the yard gate. It lightens; it never lifts, and the difference is a broken foot. A Journeyman can chain it with N-GR-17 under Eq. 4.13; a Novice should not try.

**[N-GR-44] Ferry's Lean**
Feather Fall's logic under a rescued cat: the drop eased, not stopped. Cast in the dye-works quarter wherever something heavy has to go somewhere awkward. It carries the load's edge, not the load, and the caster still sweats. Holders of N-GR-34 learn it in an afternoon; the reverse is not true.

**[N-GR-45] Barge Lift**
Eq. 4.0b in reverse over a tent peg: weight added where it is wanted, briefly. Surveyors in the lake settlements rank it above a second pair of hands. It carries the load's edge, not the load, and the caster still sweats. Compare N-GR-17, examined in the same hour and confused with it by nobody who has cast either.

**[N-GR-46] Deep's Sway**
Eq. 4.0b laid under a cart-horse's collar: weight partly cancelled for the length of the carry. Hired in the wool towns by the load; the rate is posted at the yard gate. A dropped hold is a dropped load; the examination includes one. Examiners pair it with N-GR-26 and mark the transition, not the castings.

Eq. 4.0b laid under a full cask: weight partly cancelled for the length of the carry. Turners call it by a plainer name and cast it without ceremony. The weight returns the instant the hold ends, and the trade plans for it. The working form is Eq. 8.842; the shape factor does the rest.

**[N-GR-217] Ballast Trust**
Eq. 4.0b in reverse over a stack of hides: weight added where it is wanted, briefly. Bell-ringers call it by a plainer name and cast it without ceremony. Past the ripple's reach the thing weighs what it weighs. Holders of N-GR-05 learn it in an afternoon; the reverse is not true.

**[N-GR-218] Drop of Fairs**
A whisper of Eq. 4.0b under a ferry deck so it settles rather than sways, held over `t_hold`. Standard among coopers; the examination is a loaded cart and a steep lane. A dropped hold is a dropped load; the examination includes one. Holders of N-GR-05 learn it in an afternoon; the reverse is not true.

**[N-GR-219] Quiet Plumb**
Eq. 4.0b laid under a keg: weight partly cancelled for the length of the carry, held over `t_hold`. Hired in harbour quarters by the load; the rate is posted at the yard gate. Nothing about it is flight; the directory has said so since N-GR-01. The working form, Eq. 8.845, is short; the practice behind it is not.

**[N-GR-220] Quiet Sling**
Eq. 4.0b laid along a mash tun so what should run, runs, held over `t_hold`. Taught to roofers early, since the alternative is a bad back by thirty. Nothing about it is flight; the directory has said so since N-GR-01. The working form is Eq. 8.846; the shape factor does the rest.

**[N-GR-221] Deep's Pour**
Eq. 4.0b laid under a cart-horse's collar: weight partly cancelled for the length of the carry. Stevedores in the dye-works quarter rank it above a second pair of hands. It carries the load's edge, not the load, and the caster still sweats. Everything the entry does is in Eq. 8.847; everything it does not do is in the shape factor.

**[N-GR-222] Wagon Lean**
Eq. 4.0b held just short of full cancellation under a bell-clapper, held over `t_hold`. Shepherds in a market town rank it above a second pair of hands. Past the ripple's reach the thing weighs what it weighs. Compare N-GR-29, examined in the same hour and confused with it by nobody who has cast either.

**[N-GR-223] Stone Hand**
Eq. 4.0b held just short of full cancellation under a chart weight, held over `t_hold`. Taught to stevedores early, since the alternative is a bad back by thirty. Past the ripple's reach the thing weighs what it weighs. The working form is Eq. 8.849; the shape factor does the rest.

**[N-GR-224] Lead Measure**
Eq. 4.0b held just short of full cancellation under a bell-clapper, held over `t_hold`. Cast in the salt coast wherever something heavy has to go somewhere awkward. Nothing about it is flight; the directory has said so since N-GR-01. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to N-GR-22.

Eq. 4.0b laid along an oil press so what should run, runs, held over `t_hold`. Standard among bargees; the examination is a loaded cart and a steep lane. It carries the load's edge, not the load, and the caster still sweats. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to N-GR-215.

**[N-GR-227] Anvil's Poise**
A whisper of Eq. 4.0b under a fainting patient so it settles rather than sways, held over `t_hold`. Hired in the dye-works quarter by the load; the rate is posted at the yard gate. A dropped hold is a dropped load; the examination includes one. The directory files it beside N-GR-25 and lets the two argue.

**[N-GR-228] Even Rest**
Eq. 4.0b laid under a millstone: weight partly cancelled for the length of the carry, held over `t_hold`. Standard among ropewalkers; the examination is a loaded cart and a steep lane. It carries the load's edge, not the load, and the caster still sweats. Everything the entry does is in Eq. 8.854; everything it does not do is in the shape factor.

**[N-GR-229] Pour of Rivers**
Eq. 4.0b laid under a bridge timber: weight partly cancelled for the length of the carry. Standard among masons; the examination is a loaded cart and a steep lane. Nothing about it is flight; the directory has said so since N-GR-01. N-GR-07 is the same casting on a different target, and the guilds treat them as one.

**[N-GR-230] The High Load**
Eq. 4.0b laid along a cask being racked so what should run, runs, held over `t_hold`. Standard among ferrymen; the examination is a loaded cart and a steep lane. It lightens; it never lifts, and the difference is a broken foot. N-GR-03 is the same casting on a different target, and the guilds treat them as one.

**[N-GR-231] Ferry Cradle**
Feather Fall's logic under a dropped tool: the drop eased, not stopped. Housewrights in the lake settlements rank it above a second pair of hands. The weight returns the instant the hold ends, and the trade plans for it. N-GR-26 is the same casting on a different target, and the guilds treat them as one.

**[N-GR-232] Even's Wake**
Feather Fall's logic under a dismounting rider: the drop eased, not stopped. Stevedores in the lake settlements rank it above a second pair of hands. Past the ripple's reach the thing weighs what it weighs. N-GR-05 is the same casting on a different target, and the guilds treat them as one.

Past the ripple's reach the thing weighs what it weighs. The working form, Eq. 8.859, is short; the practice behind it is not.

*Strong (`k_strong`)* — extends Eq. 4.0c.

**[N-ST-33] The Patient Shear**
Eq. 4.0c laid into a cart wheel's felloe: binding energy raised where the strain gathers, held over `t_hold`. Common in the orchard valleys, where the weather tests every bind it can find. It strengthens what is already joined; it makes nothing new. The working form is Eq. 8.860; the shape factor does the rest.

**[N-ST-34] Hull Cleave**
Eq. 4.0c pulsed through a lute's neck rather than held, to set rather than stiffen, pulsed on a period `T_pulse` rather than held. The technique behind the reputation of the fen villages for things that do not come apart. Cast on rot, it strengthens rot; the entry is not a cure. Compare N-ST-21, examined in the same hour and confused with it by nobody who has cast either.

**[N-ST-35] Wheel's Fault**
Eq. 4.0c held over a crane's jib for the length of the strain and let go the moment it passes, held over `t_hold`. Weavers in the lake settlements will not sell work that has not had it. Cast on rot, it strengthens rot; the entry is not a cure. Its working form (Eq. 8.862) is the one most apprentices learn to read first.

*Weak (`k_weak`)* — extends Eq. 4.0d. Faint by nature, as ever; every entry below says so in its own way.

**[N-WK-23] Dim's Ward**
Eq. 4.0d run in reverse over a mural: decay slowed for an evening, a week at most, held over `t_hold`. Faint, as every weak-force working is at this tier, and prized by archivists for exactly that. Nothing about it preserves; it delays, and honestly. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to N-WK-18.

**[N-WK-24] The Honest Lamp**
Faint Ward-Light's glow (N-WK-02) set over a sentry's cuff, faint and dependable, held over `t_hold`. A curer's quiet working; a tanner's open secret. Faint by nature; a Novice who expects more has misread the tier. A Journeyman can chain it with N-WK-16 under Eq. 4.13; a Novice should not try.

**[N-WK-25] The Autumn Sleep**
Faint Ward-Light's glow (N-WK-02) set over a well-head, faint and dependable. The reason the lamplighters of the sheep uplands keep better stock than their neighbours and cannot say why. The glow is a glow. It has never once been a lamp. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to N-WK-10.

**[N-WK-26] Tarnish's Ember**
Faint Ward-Light's glow (N-WK-02) set over a ferry's bow, faint and dependable, held over `t_hold`. Florists in coaching country cast it and say nothing, which is the tier's way. The glow is a glow. It has never once been a lamp. Holders of N-WK-22 learn it in an afternoon; the reverse is not true.

Its working form (Eq. 8.1064) is the one most apprentices learn to read first.

#### Journeyman (Common) — 300 entries

*Two closed-form castings held without blending under Eq. 4.13's disjoint windows; every entry names its two components by code, and the components are Novice work.*

**[J-88] The Harvest's Round** — N-ST-220 across the morning, N-EM-107 across the afternoon, with `tau_switch` spent on the walk between: the round that keeps the vintners of the sheep uplands employed. Eq. 4.13's dead time between them is where the skill lives, and the examiners time it. Its working form (Eq. 8.1240) is the one most apprentices learn to read first.

**[J-89] Ferry Duty** — N-WK-86 and N-EM-90 in strict alternation, 2 rounds to the working: a night-watch's two kinds of trouble. The two windows never overlap; a caster who lets them overlap has, briefly, attempted Adept work, and Eq. 3.3 has an opinion about that. The working form, Eq. 8.1241, is short; the practice behind it is not.

**[J-90] Winter Turn** — N-WK-72 held ready behind N-ST-116, cast only if the first goes wrong: a charcoal-burner's whole shift in two castings. The switching discipline is the technique; the components are Novice work and say so. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to J-87.

**[J-91] Harbour Shift** — N-WK-66 and N-WK-141 in strict alternation, 5 rounds to the working: a market day's two demands met by one caster. Named by the trade for the pause in the middle, which is the part that took years. Its working form (Eq. 8.1243) is the one most apprentices learn to read first.

**[J-92] Evening Errand** — N-ST-70 across the morning, N-WK-81 across the afternoon, with `tau_switch` spent on the walk between: what a coachbuilder does when the apprentice is ill. The switching discipline is the technique; the components are Novice work and say so. Its shape factor is small, and the entry is honest about it.

**[J-93] The Forge's Pair** — N-ST-156 or N-WK-32, chosen on the spot by what the day offers: a ferry crossing's two halves. A Novice can cast either half; the Journeyman is the one who does not try both at once. Eq. 8.1245 gives the number; the trade gives the name.

**[J-94] The Noon's Change** — N-EM-163 and N-WK-12 in strict alternation, 4 rounds to the working: what a roofer does when the apprentice is ill. Timed, as every entry at this tier is timed, by `tau_switch` and nothing else. The working form is Eq. 8.1246; the shape factor does the rest.

**[J-95] The Noon's Errand** — N-EM-224 and N-ST-03 in strict alternation, 5 rounds to the working: the pairing every guild of wheelwrights examines first. The two windows never overlap; a caster who lets them overlap has, briefly, attempted Adept work, and Eq. 3.3 has an opinion about that. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to J-73.

**[J-96] The Watch's Change** — N-WK-104 or N-WK-202, chosen on the spot by what the day offers: the round that keeps the ropewalkers of the sheep uplands employed. Eq. 4.13's dead time between them is where the skill lives, and the examiners time it. The working form, Eq. 8.1248, is short; the practice behind it is not.

**[J-97] The Lantern's Ledger** — N-GR-116 or N-GR-205, chosen on the spot by what the day offers: what a midwive does when the apprentice is ill. The switching discipline is the technique; the components are Novice work and say so. Its working form (Eq. 8.1249) is the one most apprentices learn to read first.

**[J-98] Wool Crossing** — N-ST-122 across the morning, N-ST-94 across the afternoon, with `tau_switch` spent on the walk between: a ferry crossing's two halves. The switching discipline is the technique; the components are Novice work and say so. The directory files it beside J-09 and lets the two argue.

**[J-99] The Harvest's Shift** — N-WK-49 across the morning, N-ST-223 across the afternoon, with `tau_switch` spent on the walk between: what a hod-carrier does when the apprentice is ill. The two windows never overlap; a caster who lets them overlap has, briefly, attempted Adept work, and Eq. 3.3 has an opinion about that. Everything the entry does is in Eq. 8.1251; everything it does not do is in the shape factor.

**[J-100] Winter Beat** — N-GR-07 across the morning, N-ST-40 across the afternoon, with `tau_switch` spent on the walk between: the round that keeps the ropewalkers of garrison towns employed. A Novice can cast either half; the Journeyman is the one who does not try both at once. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to J-85.

**[J-101] The Gate's Turn** — N-WK-202 and N-EM-95 in strict alternation, 3 rounds to the working: a hod-carrier's emergency, rehearsed until it is routine. The two windows never overlap; a caster who lets them overlap has, briefly, attempted Adept work, and Eq. 3.3 has an opinion about that. Eq. 8.1253 gives the number; the trade gives the name.

**[J-102] Orchard Reckoning** — N-EM-249 then N-EM-43, in that order and never blended: the pairing every guild of millwrights examines first. Eq. 4.13's dead time between them is where the skill lives, and the examiners time it. Its working form (Eq. 8.1254) is the one most apprentices learn to read first.

**[J-103] The Quarter's Day** — N-GR-22 across the morning, N-ST-160 across the afternoon, with `tau_switch` spent on the walk between: the round that keeps the ropewalkers of the dye-works quarter employed. Named by the trade for the pause in the middle, which is the part that took years. Eq. 8.1255 gives the number; the trade gives the name.

**[J-104] Watch Circuit** — N-WK-180 held ready behind N-GR-146, cast only if the first goes wrong: the working mining districts names its inns after. **[J-373] Wool Pair** — N-EM-72 or N-EM-82, chosen on the spot by what the day offers: a market day's two demands met by one caster. Eq. 4.13's dead time between them is where the skill lives, and the examiners time it. Its shape factor is small, and the entry is honest about it.

**[J-374] The Well's Errand** — N-GR-40 held ready behind N-GR-114, cast only if the first goes wrong: the round that keeps the cordwainers of quarry villages employed. Named by the trade for the pause in the middle, which is the part that took years. Its shape factor is small, and the entry is honest about it.

**[J-375] The Winter's Ledger** — N-EM-09 and N-WK-91 in strict alternation, 2 rounds to the working: the round that keeps the cheesemongers of the river ports employed. The two windows never overlap; a caster who lets them overlap has, briefly, attempted Adept work, and Eq. 3.3 has an opinion about that. The directory files it beside J-40 and lets the two argue.

**[J-376] Mill Duty** — N-GR-38 then N-WK-71, in that order and never blended: what a ropewalker does when the apprentice is ill. Eq. 4.13's dead time between them is where the skill lives, and the examiners time it. The directory files it beside J-38 and lets the two argue.

**[J-377] The Harvest's Chorus** — N-EM-29 across the morning, N-ST-15 across the afternoon, with `tau_switch` spent on the walk between: a ferry crossing's two halves. A Novice can cast either half; the Journeyman is the one who does not try both at once. Eq. 8.1529 gives the number; the trade gives the name.

**[J-378] The Ferry's Change** — N-EM-262 across the morning, N-ST-70 across the afternoon, with `tau_switch` spent on the walk between: the pairing every guild of couriers examines first. The switching discipline is the technique; the components are Novice work and say so. Its working form (Eq. 8.1530) is the one most apprentices learn to read first.

**[J-379] The Cellar's Circuit** — N-ST-19 across the morning, N-WK-80 across the afternoon, with `tau_switch` spent on the walk between: a night-watch's two kinds of trouble. The switching discipline is the technique; the components are Novice work and say so. Its working form (Eq. 8.1531) is the one most apprentices learn to read first.

**[J-380] Wool Chorus** — N-GR-151 or N-WK-99, chosen on the spot by what the day offers: what a well-digger does when the apprentice is ill. A Novice can cast either half; the Journeyman is the one who does not try both at once. Not to be confused with J-74, which is louder and less useful.

**[J-381] The Market's Round** — N-ST-93 across the morning, N-ST-192 across the afternoon, with `tau_switch` spent on the walk between: a market day's two demands met by one caster. The two windows never overlap; a caster who lets them overlap has, briefly, attempted Adept work, and Eq. 3.3 has an opinion about that. Not to be confused with J-40, which is louder and less useful.

**[J-382] Ferry Hour** — N-GR-75 across the morning, N-ST-205 across the afternoon, with `tau_switch` spent on the walk between: a night-watch's two kinds of trouble. Eq. 4.13's dead time between them is where the skill lives, and the examiners time it. The working form, Eq. 8.1534, is short; the practice behind it is not.

**[J-383] The Watch's Beat** — N-WK-182 and N-EM-109 in strict alternation, 4 rounds to the working: a market day's two demands met by one caster. Timed, as every entry at this tier is timed, by `tau_switch` and nothing else. Its shape factor is small, and the entry is honest about it.

**[J-384] The Lock's Beat** — N-GR-197 and N-EM-206 in strict alternation, 5 rounds to the working: a ferry crossing's two halves. A Novice can cast either half; the Journeyman is the one who does not try both at once. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to J-24.

**[J-385] The Wool's Passage** — N-ST-05 and N-GR-148 in strict alternation, 3 rounds to the working: the working garrison towns names its inns after. Named by the trade for the pause in the middle, which is the part that took years. The working form is Eq. 8.1537; the shape factor does the rest.

**[J-386] Salt Hour** — N-GR-56 or N-ST-154, chosen on the spot by what the day offers: the round that keeps the charcoal-burners of a market town employed. Timed, as every entry at this tier is timed, by `tau_switch` and nothing else. Not to be confused with J-59, which is louder and less useful.

**[J-387] The Bell's Chorus** — N-WK-23 or N-GR-106, chosen on the spot by what the day offers: a night-watch's two kinds of trouble. The switching discipline is the technique; the components are Novice work and say so. Compare J-43, examined in the same hour and confused with it by nobody who has cast either.

#### Adept (Uncommon) — 360 entries

*Sixty deepenings of each of the six solved `Chi(f1,f2)` pairs, under Eq. 4.15's `Fid^2` accounting; still no seventh pair.*

*(EM + Strong — the Flash-Forge pair, AD-01)*

**[AD-75] Shore's Crossing**
*(EM + Strong)* — heat and cohesion blended through a foundry's sprues in one pass. The Flash-Forge pair (AD-01) deepened for launderers in mining districts. Examined, like every deepening of the pair, on the `Fid^2` test: the blend must move without widening. The directory files it beside AD-01 and lets the two argue.

**[AD-76] Lantern's Turn**
*(EM + Strong)* — heat and cohesion blended through a granary in one pass. The Flash-Forge pair (AD-01) deepened for signal-crews in cathedral cities. The pair's `Chi` is the solved one; the entry only sets what the blend is pointed at. Everything the entry does is in Eq. 8.1541; everything it does not do is in the shape factor.

**[AD-77] Signal Glide**
*(EM + Strong)* — warmth walked with the hammer through a cathedral's great lamp, hard everywhere the blow is not. The Flash-Forge pair (AD-01) deepened for bakers in the salt coast. Examined, like every deepening of the pair, on the `Fid^2` test: the blend must move without widening. Eq. 8.1542 gives the number; the trade gives the name.

The Journeyman version takes twice as long and half the skill; the trade pays for the difference. The working form, Eq. 8.1760, is short; the practice behind it is not.

**[AD-296] Festival Song**
*(Gravity + Strong)* — a cathedral's great lamp held lighter and tighter through the whole of a crossing. The Sunder Weight pair (AD-04) deepened for millers in the northern marches. Fid enters squared, as it must at this tier: a wobble that a Journeyman's switching would absorb degrades both halves at once. The working form, Eq. 8.1761, is short; the practice behind it is not.

Fid enters squared, as it must at this tier: a wobble that a Journeyman's switching would absorb degrades both halves at once. The working form is Eq. 8.1839; the shape factor does the rest.

#### Artisan (Uncommon) — 260 entries

*One signature material per entry, one solved eigenvector family in `S` (Eq. 3.1e) worked through Eq. 4.16, and the neighbours it does not cover named, in the tradition of AR-01 through AR-07.*

**[AR-60] Artisan of Cypress** — solved for one cypress structure: cypress shifted in temper, grain or set from within, as the trade needs it. The neighbours it pointedly does not cover are saffron and cotton; both have cost careless holders a season's work. The tier's usual bargain: one material, wholly, and nothing else at all. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to AR-01.

**[AR-61] Artisan of Muslin** — solved for one muslin structure: flaws found and closed in muslin, or opened deliberately along the solved grain. Narrow by construction, as §3.3 requires: soap lye is a different eigenvector and hops a different world. Common in coaching country; the proof is older than the guild that holds it. Everything the entry does is in Eq. 8.1901; everything it does not do is in the shape factor.

**[AR-62] Artisan of Solder** — solved for one solder structure: flaws found and closed in solder, or opened deliberately along the solved grain. Does not extend to saffron or birch, whatever their surface likeness; each is an Eq. 4.17 backlash waiting on a caster who reasons by resemblance. Common in the lake settlements; the proof is older than the guild that holds it. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to AR-59.

**[AR-63] Artisan of Horseshoe Iron** — solved for one horseshoe iron structure: horseshoe iron shifted in temper, grain or set from within, as the trade needs it. rosewood and cuttlebone are unmodelled and are not touched, and the guild's examination includes an offer of each. Rare outside garrison towns, where the trade that needs it is. Compare AR-39, examined in the same hour and confused with it by nobody who has cast either.

**[AR-64] Artisan of Holly** — solved for one holly structure: flaws found and closed in holly, or opened deliberately along the solved grain. The neighbours it pointedly does not cover are porcelain and snakeskin; both have cost careless holders a season's work. Common in the salt coast; the proof is older than the guild that holds it. Not to be confused with AR-55, which is louder and less useful.

**[AR-65] Artisan of Sheet Tin** — solved for one sheet tin structure: flaws found and closed in sheet tin, or opened deliberately along the solved grain. Narrow by construction, as §3.3 requires: gypsum is a different eigenvector and beeswax a different world. Common in mining districts; the proof is older than the guild that holds it. Its working form (Eq. 8.1905) is the one most apprentices learn to read first.

**[AR-66] Artisan of Quill** — solved for one quill structure: purification, truing, or selective reformation of quill-based work without heat or hammer. The neighbours it pointedly does not cover are rock crystal and kaolin; both have cost careless holders a season's work. The tier's usual bargain: one material, wholly, and nothing else at all. Not to be confused with AR-31, which is louder and less useful.

**[AR-67] Artisan of Shagreen** — solved for one shagreen structure: purification, truing, or selective reformation of shagreen-based work without heat or hammer. tool steel and cedar are unmodelled and are not touched, and the guild's examination includes an offer of each. Held in the timber counties by a family line, which the directory records without comment. Its shape factor is small, and the entry is honest about it.

**[AR-68] Artisan of Coke** — solved for one coke structure: coke's structure eased, steadied or cleared of fault along one solved family. Narrow by construction, as §3.3 requires: electrum is a different eigenvector and talc a different world. Common in garrison towns; the proof is older than the guild that holds it. The directory files it beside AR-44 and lets the two argue.

**[AR-69] Artisan of Ebony** — solved for one ebony structure: ebony shifted in temper, grain or set from within, as the trade needs it. The neighbours it pointedly does not cover are lichen and river clay; both have cost careless holders a season's work. The tier's usual bargain: one material, wholly, and nothing else at all. Its shape factor is small, and the entry is honest about it.

**[AR-70] Artisan of Willow** — solved for one willow structure: willow shifted in temper, grain or set from within, as the trade needs it. baize and horseshoe iron are unmodelled and are not touched, and the guild's examination includes an offer of each. Held in the salt coast by a family line, which the directory records without comment. Not to be confused with AR-42, which is louder and less useful.

**[AR-71] Artisan of Enamel** — solved for one enamel structure: purification, truing, or selective reformation of enamel-based work without heat or hammer. vermilion and bone glue are unmodelled and are not touched, and the guild's examination includes an offer of each. Rare outside border towns, where the trade that needs it is. Not to be confused with AR-58, which is louder and less useful.

**[AR-72] Artisan of Mortar** — solved for one mortar structure: mortar's structure eased, steadied or cleared of fault along one solved family. Narrow by construction, as §3.3 requires: hazel is a different eigenvector and glue a different world. Rare outside the sheep uplands, where the trade that needs it is. Its shape factor is small, and the entry is honest about it.

**[AR-73] Artisan of Damascus** — solved for one damascus structure: damascus's structure eased, steadied or cleared of fault along one solved family. potash and lampblack are unmodelled and are not touched, and the guild's examination includes an offer of each. Rare outside the orchard valleys, where the trade that needs it is. The working form, Eq. 8.1913, is short; the practice behind it is not.

**[AR-74] Artisan of Type Metal** — solved for one type metal structure: type metal's structure eased, steadied or cleared of fault along one solved family. Does not extend to straw or crepe, whatever their surface likeness; each is an Eq. 4.17 backlash waiting on a caster who reasons by resemblance. Held in coaching country by a family line, which the directory records without comment. The working form is Eq. 8.1914; the shape factor does the rest.

**[AR-75] Artisan of Fir** — solved for one fir structure: purification, truing, or selective reformation of fir-based work without heat or hammer. whale oil and baize are unmodelled and are not touched, and the guild's examination includes an offer of each. Rare outside mining districts, where the trade that needs it is. Its working form (Eq. 8.1915) is the one most apprentices learn to read first.

**[AR-76] Artisan of Lichen** — solved for one lichen structure: lichen worked to a finish no tool reaches, one eigenvector deep. milk and gravel are unmodelled and are not touched, and the guild's examination includes an offer of each. Rare outside the timber counties, where the trade that needs it is. Everything the entry does is in Eq. 8.1994; everything it does not do is in the shape factor.

**[AR-155] Artisan of Cider** — solved for one cider structure: cider's structure eased, steadied or cleared of fault along one solved family. The neighbours it pointedly does not cover are lime wood and sponge; both have cost careless holders a season's work. The tier's usual bargain: one material, wholly, and nothing else at all. The working form is Eq. 8.1995; the shape factor does the rest.

The working form is Eq. 8.2072; the shape factor does the rest.

**[AR-233] Artisan of Vinegar** — solved for one vinegar structure: flaws found and closed in vinegar, or opened deliberately along the solved grain. Narrow by construction, as §3.3 requires: ebony is a different eigenvector and woad a different world. Rare outside the lake settlements, where the trade that needs it is. Compare AR-21, examined in the same hour and confused with it by nobody who has cast either.

**[AR-234] Artisan of Varnish** — solved for one varnish structure: flaws found and closed in varnish, or opened deliberately along the solved grain. Does not extend to pumice or holly, whatever their surface likeness; each is an Eq. 4.17 backlash waiting on a caster who reasons by resemblance. The tier's usual bargain: one material, wholly, and nothing else at all. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to AR-16.

**[AR-310] Artisan of Chain** — solved for one chain structure: flaws found and closed in chain, or opened deliberately along the solved grain. Narrow by construction, as §3.3 requires: millstone grit is a different eigenvector and steel a different world. Common in the lake settlements; the proof is older than the guild that holds it. Not to be confused with AR-41, which is louder and less useful.

**[AR-311] Artisan of Britannia** — solved for one britannia structure: flaws found and closed in britannia, or opened deliberately along the solved grain. Narrow by construction, as §3.3 requires: rennet is a different eigenvector and white metal a different world. The tier's usual bargain: one material, wholly, and nothing else at all. The working form is Eq. 8.2151; the shape factor does the rest.

**[AR-312] Artisan of Satin** — solved for one satin structure: satin worked to a finish no tool reaches, one eigenvector deep. Does not extend to whetstone or sap, whatever their surface likeness; each is an Eq. 4.17 backlash waiting on a caster who reasons by resemblance. Rare outside canal country, where the trade that needs it is. Eq. 8.2152 gives the number; the trade gives the name.

**[AR-313] Artisan of Tombac** — solved for one tombac structure: tombac's structure eased, steadied or cleared of fault along one solved family. cork and osier are unmodelled and are not touched, and the guild's examination includes an offer of each. Rare outside the orchard valleys, where the trade that needs it is. Its working form (Eq. 8.2153) is the one most apprentices learn to read first.

**[AR-314] Artisan of Yeast** — solved for one yeast structure: flaws found and closed in yeast, or opened deliberately along the solved grain. Narrow by construction, as §3.3 requires: lead glass is a different eigenvector and sizing a different world. Rare outside the lake settlements, where the trade that needs it is. The working form, Eq. 8.2154, is short; the practice behind it is not.

**[AR-315] Artisan of Jute** — solved for one jute structure: flaws found and closed in jute, or opened deliberately along the solved grain. The neighbours it pointedly does not cover are thread and willow; both have cost careless holders a season's work. The tier's usual bargain: one material, wholly, and nothing else at all. Not to be confused with AR-05, which is louder and less useful.

**[AR-316] Artisan of Boxwood** — solved for one boxwood structure: flaws found and closed in boxwood, or opened deliberately along the solved grain. Does not extend to frieze or sheet copper, whatever their surface likeness; each is an Eq. 4.17 backlash waiting on a caster who reasons by resemblance. Common in coaching country; the proof is older than the guild that holds it. The directory files it beside AR-50 and lets the two argue.

**[AR-317] Artisan of Brocade** — solved for one brocade structure: brocade worked to a finish no tool reaches, one eigenvector deep. larch and clay slip are unmodelled and are not touched, and the guild's examination includes an offer of each. The tier's usual bargain: one material, wholly, and nothing else at all. The working form is Eq. 8.2157; the shape factor does the rest.

**[AR-318] Artisan of Bone Ash** — solved for one bone ash structure: bone ash's structure eased, steadied or cleared of fault along one solved family. Does not extend to lard or wheat, whatever their surface likeness; each is an Eq. 4.17 backlash waiting on a caster who reasons by resemblance. Rare outside a market town, where the trade that needs it is. Eq. 8.2158 gives the number; the trade gives the name.

**[AR-319] Artisan of Rawhide** — solved for one rawhide structure: rawhide's structure eased, steadied or cleared of fault along one solved family. Does not extend to sea salt or pitch, whatever their surface likeness; each is an Eq. 4.17 backlash waiting on a caster who reasons by resemblance. Common in the hill parishes; the proof is older than the guild that holds it. Eq. 8.2159 gives the number; the trade gives the name.

#### Master (Rare) — 160 entries

*Complete-`S` workings (Eq. 4.18–4.19), each naming the sign it was inscribed with.*

**[M-35] Surveyor's Peace** — the slow ruin of a river's lock-gates arrested (`s = -1`) across every material in it for the length of a contract, a Master's standing presence worn like the place's own held breath. Retained, where it is retained, as infrastructure, and accounted so. Its working form (Eq. 8.2160) is the one most apprentices learn to read first.

**[M-36] Vintner's Keeping** — a dockyard's rope-store aged or steadied to order, any material, no proof per material needed, a Master's standing presence worn like the place's own held breath. Civic first, extraordinary later, spectacle never; the entry keeps M-02's founding observation. The working form is Eq. 8.2161; the shape factor does the rest.

**[M-37] Bridgemaster's Order** — the slow ruin of a siege line arrested (`s = -1`) across every material in it for the length of a contract, a Master's standing presence worn like the place's own held breath. Towns that can keep a holder of it stop mentioning the fact, which is how the directory knows they can. Not to be confused with M-26, which is louder and less useful.

**[M-192] Harbourmaster's Mercy** — one quantity of matter in a salt-works rewritten across the complete eigenbasis (Eq. 4.18) into what the work requires, a Master's standing presence worn like the place's own held breath. Retained, where it is retained, as infrastructure, and accounted so. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to M-01.

**[M-193] Harbourmaster's Watch** — one quantity of matter in a royal mint rewritten across the complete eigenbasis (Eq. 4.18) into what the work requires, a Master's standing presence worn like the place's own held breath. Civic first, extraordinary later, spectacle never; the entry keeps M-02's founding observation. Compare M-14, examined in the same hour and confused with it by nobody who has cast either.

**[M-194] Warden's Mercy** — a granary district aged or steadied to order, any material, no proof per material needed, a Master's standing presence worn like the place's own held breath. The complete `S` behind it is the tier's whole definition; the entry only says what it was pointed at. Its shape factor is small, and the entry is honest about it.

*Each entry names its proven geometry and ends at `eps_valid(R_proven)` (Eq. 4.20–4.21); catalogable only because the geometry is a published class.*

#### Legend (A Handful Across Recorded History) — 40 entries

*Standing works under Eq. 4.22–4.23; every one an institution.*

**[LG-19] The Raised Causeway** — a river carried through the twin ports in a standing channel of eased weight. Every entry at this tier is an institution, because Eq. 4.22–4.23's upkeep permits nothing less; the maintenance interval is the operative constraint. The directory files it beside LG-10 and lets the two argue.

**[LG-20] The Long Ford** — a river carried through the iron valleys in a standing channel of eased weight. Recorded by the directory as it records all Legends: by what it costs to keep. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to LG-18.

**[LG-21] The Standing Lamp** — a standing light over the salt coast that three generations have navigated by and none has had to relight. Its hereditary order keeps the `t_drift` bookkeeping; the working itself is one line. Everything the entry does is in Eq. 8.2442; everything it does not do is in the shape factor.

**[LG-22] The Held Tide** — a bound star above the vineyard duchies, its shell re-inscribed on `t_recert`. Every entry at this tier is an institution, because Eq. 4.22–4.23's upkeep permits nothing less; the maintenance interval is the operative constraint. Its shape factor is small, and the entry is honest about it.

**[LG-23] The Quiet Pass** — a standing ease along the whole length of the cathedral city, resurveyed on a `t_drift` cadence. Recorded by the directory as it records all Legends: by what it costs to keep. Related, in practice if not in mechanism, to LG-08.

**[LG-24] The Winter Bridge** — a pass over the old frontier where the climb weighs half what geology insists. The working form, Eq. 8.2445, is short; the practice behind it is not.

#### Beyond Legend (Historical Fragments) — 21 entries

*Fragments toward the four paths of §3.3, quantified by Eq. 4.24–4.27.*

**[AS-11] The Fifth Servant** — a Tetrarch fragment: a signature working chaining the solved pairs so tightly that the seams showed only to Eq. 4.24's audit. Quantified after the fact by Eq. 4.24, and catalogued, as every entry at this tier is, for what it proves about the distance rather than as a route across it. Everything the entry does is in Eq. 8.2480; everything it does not do is in the shape factor.

**[AS-12] The Unfinished Alloy** — a Demiurge fragment: an aspirant who originated, by a proven rule, a substance no assay had seen and no assay could fault. Quantified after the fact by Eq. 4.25, and catalogued, as every entry at this tier is, for what it proves about the distance rather than as a route across it. The working form is Eq. 8.2481; the shape factor does the rest.

**[AS-13] The Outer Ring** — a Cosmographer fragment: a standing work's `t_dom` pressed past Legend's until the resurveys outran the surveyors.
